To place bowl at plate center: eef = plate_center - offset = (0.049 - 0.022, 0.190 + 0.051) = (0.027, 0.241). Hmm y=0.241 — near the y=0.27 IK wall. Risky but doable? Memory notes |y|>0.27 walls. 0.241 is under.

Hmm, but the offset estimate could be biased: the bowl centroid from the z-band includes only visible upper surface of the bowl (the bowl is seen from an oblique camera; the visible parts are biased toward the camera side, i.e., toward -x/-y?). Camera at agentview position... The bowl hangs tilted. Risky.

Alternative approach: rather than trusting the offset, place so that the bowl overlaps the plate center as verified by a second measurement after descending. Since I can measure the bowl centroid any time, I can descend partway, re-measure, correct.

But descent in xy-corrected fashion: I can do move_to with x+dy corrections.

So bowl top rim ≈ eef z (fingers at eef level? In Panda, eef_pos is at the hand frame between fingers; fingertips extend ~0.045 below eef... hmm, actually in LIBERO the eef site is roughly at the fingertips).

Hmm — for grasp verification: eef z at pick was 1.0913-1.0952, and the bowl rim was ~1.10-1.11. So eef z ≈ rim z ≈ bowl top. Therefore bowl bottom ≈ eef z - 0.06.

Current eef z=1.238 → bowl bottom ≈ 1.178. To rest the bowl on the plate (surface 0.907), the eef should descend to ≈ 0.907 + 0.06 = 0.967 (plus small margin). But such a low eef (0.967) with the gripper body — the gripper will be near the table, fine.

Hmm, but careful: if the bowl is pinched at the rim and I descend until the bowl bottom touches the plate, the further descent is blocked, and OSC may stall. OSX stalls are fine (~1cm above).

Plan: descend to z=0.99 and release → bowl drops ~2cm. Should settle upright.

Actually, even better: after reaching xy over the plate, descend slowly to z=0.98 with step_clip 0.008. If the bowl bottom contacts, the servo will stall around 1.00. Then release.

Let's first do the xy correction move: move_to([0.027, 0.235, 1.24], gripper 1, step_clip 0.012) — that moves xy by (-0.021,+0.054) at same z. Then re-measure. Hmm, why not just directly combine the xy correction with the descent? Because the offset measurement might be biased; a mid-level re-check is cheap. But each step costs. Let me do the xy correction at carry height, re-measure, then descend.

Actually there's a subtlety: The bowl's visible centroid in agentview depends on the camera geometry, and both the bowl and the plate are measured with the same camera, so the systematic projection bias partially cancels when comparing centroid-to-centroid... not exactly (different heights → the parallax bias differs). At the plate, we want the bowl's PROJECTION to be at the plate center? No — we want the bowl's actual center to be over the plate center. The projection bias for the bowl at z~1.2 vs plate at z~0.91: an oblique camera produces a shifted centroid... The centroid of a disc at higher z projects further from the camera's nadir. Since we compute world_xyz per pixel via depth (not just projection), back-projection accounts for height. The centroid bias is mainly because we only see part of the bowl surface (occluded lower/back parts). The bowl is symmetric-ish; visible top interior + rim... The centroid may be biased toward the side facing the camera. 

Camera: agentview is above-front. If the camera is at -x (front of the robot? no). Let's determine: the robot base is at image top-center (background). Objects with larger x appear lower in the image (closer to the camera). So the camera is at large +x looking toward -x. So visible faces of objects are biased toward +x. So the measured centroid is biased toward +x (i.e., toward the camera). This means true bowl center ≈ measured centroid - (bias in +x), (maybe ~1-2cm).

So plate center measurement (a flat disc, symmetric, so minimal bias other than rim inclusion) ≈ true. Fine.

To reduce risk: after descending to z~1.10, I'll compare the bowl centroid with the plate center using region mode again, and correct so that the difference is small. Both measured at different heights but geometry handled.

Let's proceed: move to the corrected xy at carry height.

The far-side interior wall's inner surface faces away → hidden by the near rim.\n\nSo the visible interior is biased toward the NEAR side = +x. So measured centroid is biased toward +x relative to the true bowl center. Hence the true bowl center is at SMALLER x than 0.066 → more like 0.055 or less. Which coincidentally means the bowl is more centered over the plate (0.049).\n\nThat supports NOT correcting much. Also the gripper occludes part.\n\nAlso, note the physical geometry: the bowl hangs from the gripper pinching its rim. The pinch point is on the rim. Which side? eef xy (0.029, 0.224) and the bowl appears offset to +x and -y from eef.
